<sup>27</sup> Depart from evil, and do good.  
&emsp;Live securely forever.  
<sup>28</sup> For the LORD loves justice,  
&emsp;and doesn’t forsake his saints.  
&emsp;They are preserved forever,  
&emsp;but the children of the wicked shall be cut off.  
<sup>29</sup> The righteous shall inherit the land,  
&emsp;and live in it forever.

<sup>30</sup> The mouth of the righteous talks of wisdom.  
&emsp;His tongue speaks justice.  
<sup>31</sup> The law of his God is in his heart.  
&emsp;None of his steps shall slide.  
<sup>32</sup> The wicked watch the righteous,  
&emsp;and seek to kill him.  
<sup>33</sup> The LORD will not leave him in his hand,  
&emsp;nor condemn him when he is judged.  
<sup>34</sup> Wait for the LORD, and keep his way,  
&emsp;and he will exalt you to inherit the land.  
&emsp;When the wicked are cut off, you shall see it.

<sup>35</sup> I have seen the wicked in great power,  
&emsp;~~spreading himself like a green tree in its native soil~~ <ins>exalted and lifted up like a flourishing cedar</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c146"></a>[c146](#c146)</sup>.  
<sup>36</sup> But he passed away, and behold, he was not.  
&emsp;Yes, I sought him, but he could not be found.  
<sup>37</sup> Mark the perfect man, and see the upright,  
&emsp;for there is a future for the man of peace.  
<sup>38</sup> As for transgressors, they shall be destroyed together.  
&emsp;The future of the wicked shall be cut off.  
<sup>39</sup> But the salvation of the righteous is from the LORD.  
&emsp;He is their ~~stronghold~~ <ins>protector</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c147"></a>[c147](#c147)</sup> in the time of trouble.  
<sup>40</sup> The LORD helps them and rescues them.  
&emsp;He rescues them from the wicked and saves them,  
&emsp;because they have taken refuge in him.

## Psalm 38

*A Psalm by David, for a memorial.*

<sup>1</sup> LORD, don’t rebuke me in your wrath,  
&emsp;neither chasten me in your hot displeasure.  
<sup>2</sup> For your arrows have pierced me,  
&emsp;your hand presses hard on me.  
<sup>3</sup> There is no soundness in my flesh because of your indignation,  
&emsp;neither is there ~~any health in my bones~~ <ins>any peace in my bones</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c148"></a>[c148](#c148)</sup> because of my sin.  
<sup>4</sup> For my iniquities have gone over my head.  
&emsp;As a heavy burden, they are too heavy for me.  
<sup>5</sup> My wounds are loathsome and corrupt  
&emsp;because of my foolishness.  
<sup>6</sup> I am in pain and bowed down greatly.  
&emsp;I go mourning all day long.  
<sup>7</sup> For my ~~waist is filled~~ <ins>loins are filled</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c149"></a>[c149](#c149)</sup> with burning.  
&emsp;There is no soundness in my flesh.  
<sup>8</sup> I am ~~faint~~ <ins>afflicted</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c151"></a>[c151](#c151)</sup> and ~~severely bruised~~ <ins>humbled exceedingly</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c152"></a>[c152](#c152)</sup>.  
&emsp;~~I have groaned~~ <ins>I have roared</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c150"></a>[c150](#c150)</sup> by reason of ~~the anguish of my heart~~ <ins>the groaning of my heart</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c153"></a>[c153](#c153)</sup>.  
<sup>9</sup> Lord, all my desire is before you.  
&emsp;My groaning is not hidden from you.  
<sup>10</sup> My heart throbs.  
&emsp;My strength fails me.  
&emsp;As for the light of my eyes, it has also left me.  
<sup>11</sup> My lovers and my friends stand aloof from my plague.  
&emsp;My kinsmen stand far away.  
<sup>12</sup> They also who seek after my life lay snares.  
&emsp;Those who seek my hurt speak ~~mischievous things~~ <ins>treacherous things</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c154"></a>[c154](#c154)</sup>,  
&emsp;and meditate deceits all day long.  
<sup>13</sup> But I, as a deaf man, don’t hear.  
&emsp;I am as a mute man who doesn’t open his mouth.  
<sup>14</sup> Yes, I am as a man who doesn’t hear,  
&emsp;in whose mouth are no reproofs.  
<sup>15</sup> For I hope in you, LORD.  
&emsp;You will answer, Lord my God.  
<sup>16</sup> For I said, “Don’t let them gloat over me,  
&emsp;or exalt themselves over me when my foot slips.”  
<sup>17</sup> For I am ready to fall.  
&emsp;My pain is continually before me.  
<sup>18</sup> For I will declare my iniquity.  
&emsp;~~I will be sorry for my sin.~~ <ins>I am anxious about my sin.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c155"></a>[c155](#c155)</sup>  
<sup>19</sup> But my enemies are vigorous and many.  
&emsp;Those who hate me without reason are numerous.  
<sup>20</sup> They who give evil for good are also adversaries to me,  
&emsp;because I follow what is good.  
<sup>21</sup> Don’t forsake me, LORD.  
&emsp;My God, don’t be far from me.  
<sup>22</sup> Hurry to help me,  
&emsp;Lord, my salvation.

## Psalm 39

*For the Chief Musician. For Jeduthun. A Psalm by David.*

<sup>1</sup> I said, “I will watch my ways, so that I don’t sin with my tongue.  
&emsp;I will keep my mouth with a bridle while the wicked is before me.”  
<sup>2</sup> I was mute with silence.  
&emsp;I held my peace, even from good.  
&emsp;My sorrow was stirred.  
<sup>3</sup> My heart was hot within me.  
&emsp;While I meditated, the fire burned.  
I spoke with my tongue:  
&emsp;<sup>4</sup> “LORD, show me my end,  
&emsp;what is the measure of my days.  
&emsp;Let me know how frail I am.  
<sup>5</sup> Behold, you have made my days hand widths.  
&emsp;My lifetime is as nothing before you.  
Surely every man stands ~~as a breath~~ <ins>as total vanity</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c156"></a>[c156](#c156)</sup>.” *Selah.*  
<sup>6</sup> “Surely every man ~~walks like a shadow~~ <ins>passes by as an image</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c157"></a>[c157](#c157)</sup>.  
&emsp;Surely they busy themselves in vain.  
&emsp;He heaps up, and doesn’t know who shall gather.  
<sup>7</sup> Now, Lord, what do I wait for?  
&emsp;My hope is in you.  
<sup>8</sup> Deliver me from all my transgressions.  
&emsp;Don’t make me the reproach of the foolish.  
<sup>9</sup> I was mute.  
&emsp;I didn’t open my mouth,  
&emsp;because you did it.  
<sup>10</sup> Remove your scourge away from me.  
&emsp;I am overcome by the blow of your hand.  
<sup>11</sup> When you rebuke and correct man for iniquity,  
&emsp;you consume his wealth like a moth.  
Surely every man is ~~but a breath~~ <ins>vanity</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c158"></a>[c158](#c158)</sup>.” *Selah.*  
<sup>12</sup> “Hear my prayer, LORD, and give ear to my cry.  
&emsp;Don’t ~~be silent at~~ <ins>be deaf to</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c159"></a>[c159](#c159)</sup> my tears.  
For I am a stranger with you,  
&emsp;a foreigner, as all my fathers were.  
<sup>13</sup> Oh ~~spare me~~ <ins>turn away from me</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c160"></a>[c160](#c160)</sup>, that I may recover strength,  
&emsp;before I go away and exist no more.”

## Psalm 40

*For the Chief Musician. A Psalm by David.*

<sup>1</sup> I waited patiently for the LORD.  
&emsp;He turned to me, and heard my cry.  
<sup>2</sup> He brought me up also out of ~~a horrible pit~~ <ins>a pit of misery</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c161"></a>[c161](#c161)</sup>,  
&emsp;out of the miry clay.  
He set my feet on a rock,  
&emsp;and gave me a firm place to stand.  
<sup>3</sup> He has put a new song in my mouth, even praise to our God.  
&emsp;Many shall see it, and fear, and shall trust in the LORD.  
<sup>4</sup> Blessed is the man who makes the LORD his trust,  
&emsp;and doesn’t respect the proud, nor such as turn away to lies.  
<sup>5</sup> Many, LORD, my God, are the wonderful works which you have done,  
&emsp;and your thoughts which are toward us.  
They can’t be declared back to you.  
&emsp;If I would declare and speak of them, they are more than can be counted.  
<sup>6</sup> Sacrifice and offering you didn’t desire.  
&emsp;You have opened my ears.  
&emsp;You have not required burnt offering and sin offering.  
<sup>7</sup> Then I said, “Behold, I have come.  
&emsp;It is written about me in the book in the scroll.  
<sup>8</sup> ~~I delight to do~~ <ins>I wish to do</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c162"></a>[c162](#c162)</sup> your will, my God.  
&emsp;Yes, your law is within my ~~heart~~ <ins>inner parts</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c163"></a>[c163](#c163)</sup>.”  
<sup>9</sup> I have proclaimed glad news of righteousness in the great assembly.  
&emsp;Behold, I will not seal my lips, LORD, you know.  
<sup>10</sup> I have not hidden your righteousness within my heart.  
&emsp;I have declared your faithfulness and your salvation.  
&emsp;I have not concealed your loving kindness and your truth from the great assembly.  
<sup>11</sup> Don’t withhold your tender mercies from me, LORD.  
&emsp;Let your loving kindness and your truth continually preserve me.  
<sup>12</sup> For innumerable evils have surrounded me.  
&emsp;My iniquities have overtaken me, so that I am not able to look up.  
They are more than the hairs of my head.  
&emsp;My heart has failed me.  
<sup>13</sup> Be pleased, LORD, to deliver me.  
&emsp;Hurry to help me, LORD.  
<sup>14</sup> Let them be disappointed and confounded together who seek after my soul to destroy it.  
&emsp;Let them be turned backward and brought to dishonor who delight in my hurt.  
<sup>15</sup> Let them be desolate by reason of their shame that tell me, “Aha! Aha!”  
<sup>16</sup> Let all those who seek you rejoice and be glad in you.  
&emsp;Let such as love your salvation say continually, “Let the LORD be exalted!”  
<sup>17</sup> But I am poor and needy.  
&emsp;May the Lord think about me.  
You are my help and my deliverer.  
&emsp;Don’t delay, my God.

## Psalm 41

*For the Chief Musician. A Psalm by David.*

<sup>1</sup> Blessed is he who considers the poor.  
&emsp;The LORD will deliver him in the day of evil.  
<sup>2</sup> The LORD will preserve him, and keep him alive.  
&emsp;He shall be blessed on the earth,  
&emsp;and he will not surrender him to the will of his enemies.  
<sup>3</sup> The LORD will sustain him on his sickbed,  
&emsp;and restore him from his bed of illness.  
<sup>4</sup> I said, “LORD, have mercy on me!  
&emsp;Heal me, for I have sinned against you.”  
<sup>5</sup> My enemies speak evil against me:  
&emsp;“When will he die, and his name perish?”  
<sup>6</sup> If he comes to see me, he speaks falsehood.  
&emsp;His heart gathers iniquity to itself.  
&emsp;When he goes abroad, he tells it.  
<sup>7</sup> All who hate me whisper together against me.  
&emsp;They imagine the worst for me.  
<sup>8</sup> “An evil disease”, they say, “has afflicted him.  
&emsp;Now that he lies he shall rise up no more.”  
<sup>9</sup> Yes, my own familiar friend, in whom I trusted,  
&emsp;who ate bread with me,  
&emsp;has lifted up his heel against me.

<sup>10</sup> But you, LORD, have mercy on me, and raise me up,  
&emsp;that I may repay them.  
<sup>11</sup> By this I know that you delight in me,  
&emsp;because my enemy doesn’t triumph over me.  
<sup>12</sup> As for me, you uphold me in my integrity,  
&emsp;and set me in your presence forever.

<sup>13</sup> Blessed be the LORD, the God of Israel,  
&emsp;from everlasting and to everlasting!  
Amen and amen.

## Psalm 42

**BOOK 2**

*For the Chief Musician. A contemplation by the sons of Korah.*

<sup>1</sup> As the deer pants for the water brooks,  
&emsp;so my soul pants after you, God.[^17]  
<sup>2</sup> My soul thirsts for God, for the living God.  
&emsp;When shall I come and appear before God?  
<sup>3</sup> My tears have been my food day and night,  
&emsp;while they continually ask me, “Where is your God?”  
<sup>4</sup> These things I remember, and pour out my soul within me,  
&emsp;how I used to go ~~with the crowd, and led them to God’s house~~ <ins>to the place of the wonderful tabernacle, to God’s house</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c164"></a>[c164](#c164)</sup>,  
&emsp;with the voice of joy and praise, a multitude keeping a holy day.  
<sup>5</sup> Why are you in despair, my soul?  
&emsp;Why are you disturbed within me?  
Hope in God!  
&emsp;For I shall still praise him for the saving help of his presence.  
<sup>6</sup> My God, my soul is in despair within me.  
&emsp;Therefore I remember you from the land of the Jordan,  
&emsp;the heights of Hermon, from the hill Mizar.  
<sup>7</sup> Deep calls to deep at the noise of your waterfalls.  
&emsp;~~All your waves~~ <ins>All your whirlpools</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c165"></a>[c165](#c165)</sup> and your billows have swept over me.

<sup>8</sup> The LORD[^18] will command his loving kindness in the daytime.  
&emsp;In the night his song shall be with me:  
&emsp;a prayer to the God of my life.  
<sup>9</sup> I will ask God, my ~~rock~~ <ins>protector</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c166"></a>[c166](#c166)</sup>, “Why have you forgotten me?  
&emsp;Why do I go mourning because of the oppression of the enemy?”  
<sup>10</sup> ~~As with a sword in my bones~~ <ins>As my bones are broken</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c167"></a>[c167](#c167)</sup>, my adversaries reproach me,  
&emsp;while they continually ask me, “Where is your God?”  
<sup>11</sup> Why are you in despair, my soul?  
&emsp;Why are you disturbed within me?  
Hope in God! For I shall still praise him,  
&emsp;the saving help of my countenance, and my God.

## Psalm 43

<sup>1</sup> Vindicate me, God, and plead my cause against an ungodly nation.  
&emsp;Oh, deliver me from deceitful and wicked men.  
<sup>2</sup> For you are the God of my strength. Why have you rejected me?  
&emsp;Why do I go mourning because of the oppression of the enemy?  
<sup>3</sup> Oh, send out your light and your truth.  
&emsp;Let them lead me.  
&emsp;Let them bring me to your holy hill,  
&emsp;to your tents.  
<sup>4</sup> Then I will go to the altar of God,  
&emsp;to God, my exceeding joy.  
I will praise you on the harp, God, my God.  
<sup>5</sup> Why are you in despair, my soul?  
&emsp;Why are you disturbed within me?  
Hope in God!  
&emsp;For I shall still praise him:  
&emsp;my Savior, my helper, and my God.

## Psalm 44

*For the Chief Musician. By the sons of Korah. A contemplative psalm.*

<sup>1</sup> We have heard with our ears, God;  
&emsp;our fathers have told us what work you did in their days,  
&emsp;in the days of old.  
<sup>2</sup> You drove out the nations with your hand,  
&emsp;but you planted them.  
You afflicted the peoples,  
&emsp;but you spread them abroad.  
<sup>3</sup> For they didn’t get the land in possession by their own sword,  
&emsp;neither did their own arm save them;  
but your right hand, your arm, and the light of your face,  
&emsp;because you were favorable to them.  
<sup>4</sup> God, you are my King.  
&emsp;Command victories for Jacob!  
<sup>5</sup> Through you, we will push down our adversaries.  
&emsp;Through your name, we will tread down those who rise up against us.  
<sup>6</sup> For I will not trust in my bow,  
&emsp;neither will my sword save me.  
<sup>7</sup> But you have saved us from our adversaries,  
&emsp;and have shamed those who hate us.  
<sup>8</sup> In God we have made our boast all day long.  
&emsp;We will give thanks to your name forever. *Selah.*

<sup>9</sup> But now you rejected us, and brought us to dishonor,  
&emsp;and don’t go out with our armies.  
<sup>10</sup> You make us turn back from the adversary.  
&emsp;Those who hate us take plunder for themselves.  
<sup>11</sup> You have made us like sheep for food,  
&emsp;and have scattered us among the nations.  
<sup>12</sup> You sell your people for nothing,  
&emsp;and have gained nothing from their sale.  
<sup>13</sup> You make us a reproach to our neighbors,  
&emsp;a scoffing and a derision to those who are around us.  
<sup>14</sup> You make us a byword among the nations,  
&emsp;a shaking of the head among the peoples.  
<sup>15</sup> All day long my dishonor is before me,  
&emsp;and shame covers my face,  
&emsp;<sup>16</sup> at the taunt of one who reproaches and verbally abuses,  
&emsp;because of the enemy and the avenger.  
<sup>17</sup> All this has come on us,  
&emsp;yet we haven’t forgotten you.  
&emsp;We haven’t been false to your covenant.  
<sup>18</sup> Our heart has not turned back,  
&emsp;neither have our steps strayed from your path,  
&emsp;<sup>19</sup> though you have crushed us in the haunt of jackals,  
&emsp;and covered us with the shadow of death.  
<sup>20</sup> If we have forgotten the name of our God,  
&emsp;or spread out our hands to a strange god,  
&emsp;<sup>21</sup> won’t God search this out?  
&emsp;For he knows the secrets of the heart.  
<sup>22</sup> Yes, for your sake we are killed all day long.  
&emsp;We are regarded as sheep for the slaughter.  
<sup>23</sup> Wake up!  
&emsp;Why do you sleep, Lord?[^19]  
Arise!  
&emsp;Don’t reject us forever.  
<sup>24</sup> Why do you hide your face,  
&emsp;and forget our ~~affliction~~ <ins>poverty</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c168"></a>[c168](#c168)</sup> and our oppression?  
<sup>25</sup> For our soul is bowed down to the dust.  
&emsp;Our body clings to the earth.  
<sup>26</sup> Rise up to help us.  
&emsp;Redeem us for your loving kindness’ sake.

## Psalm 45

*For the Chief Musician. Set to “The Lilies.” A contemplation by the sons of Korah. A wedding song.*

<sup>1</sup> My heart overflows with ~~a noble theme~~ <ins>a good word</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c169"></a>[c169](#c169)</sup>.  
&emsp;I recite my verses for the king.  
&emsp;My tongue is like the pen of a ~~skillful writer~~ <ins>swift-writing scribe</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c170"></a>[c170](#c170)</sup>.  
<sup>2</sup> You are the most excellent of the sons of men.  
&emsp;Grace has anointed your lips,  
&emsp;therefore God has blessed you forever.  
<sup>3</sup> Strap your sword on your thigh, O mighty one,  
&emsp;in your splendor and your majesty.  
<sup>4</sup> In your majesty ride on victoriously on behalf of truth, humility, and righteousness.  
&emsp;Let your right hand display awesome deeds.  
<sup>5</sup> Your arrows are sharp.  
&emsp;The nations fall under you, with arrows in the heart of the king’s enemies.  
<sup>6</sup> Your throne, God, is forever and ever.  
&emsp;A scepter of equity is the scepter of your kingdom.  
<sup>7</sup> You have loved righteousness, and hated wickedness.  
&emsp;Therefore God, your God, has anointed you with the oil of gladness above your fellows.  
<sup>8</sup> All your garments smell like myrrh, aloes, and cassia.  
&emsp;Out of ivory palaces stringed instruments have made you glad.  
<sup>9</sup> Kings’ daughters are among your honorable women.  
&emsp;At your right hand the queen stands in gold of Ophir.  
<sup>10</sup> Listen, daughter, consider, and turn your ear.  
&emsp;Forget your own people, and also your father’s house.  
&emsp;<sup>11</sup> So the king will desire your beauty,  
&emsp;honor him, for he is your lord.  
<sup>12</sup> The daughter of Tyre comes with a gift.  
&emsp;The rich among the people entreat your favor.  
<sup>13</sup> The princess inside is all glorious.  
&emsp;Her clothing is interwoven with gold.  
<sup>14</sup> She shall be led to the king in embroidered work.  
&emsp;The virgins, her companions who follow her, shall be brought to you.  
<sup>15</sup> With gladness and rejoicing they shall be led.  
&emsp;They shall enter into the king’s palace.  
<sup>16</sup> Your sons will take the place of your fathers.  
&emsp;You shall make them princes in all the earth.  
<sup>17</sup> I will make your name to be remembered in all generations.  
&emsp;Therefore the peoples shall give you thanks forever and ever.

## Psalm 46

*For the Chief Musician. By the sons of Korah. According to Alamoth.[^20]*

<sup>1</sup> God is our refuge and strength,  
&emsp;a very present help in trouble.  
<sup>2</sup> Therefore we won’t be afraid, though the earth changes,  
&emsp;though the mountains are shaken into the heart of the seas;  
&emsp;<sup>3</sup> though its waters roar and are troubled,  
&emsp;though the mountains tremble with their swelling. *Selah.*

<sup>4</sup> There is a river, the streams of which make the city of God glad,  
&emsp;the holy place of the tents of the Most High.  
<sup>5</sup> God is within her. She shall not be moved.  
&emsp;God will help her at dawn.  
<sup>6</sup> The nations raged. The kingdoms were moved.  
&emsp;He lifted his voice and the earth melted.  
<sup>7</sup> The LORD of Armies is with us.  
&emsp;The God of Jacob is our refuge. *Selah.*

<sup>8</sup> Come, see the LORD’s works,  
&emsp;what ~~desolations~~ <ins>wonders</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c171"></a>[c171](#c171)</sup> he has made on the earth.  
<sup>9</sup> He makes wars cease to the end of the earth.  
&emsp;He breaks the bow, and shatters the spear.  
&emsp;He burns the ~~chariots~~ <ins>shields</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c172"></a>[c172](#c172)</sup> in the fire.  
<sup>10</sup> “Be still, and know that I am God.  
&emsp;I will be exalted among the nations.  
&emsp;I will be exalted on the earth.”  
<sup>11</sup> The LORD of Armies is with us.  
&emsp;The God of Jacob is our refuge. *Selah.*

## Psalm 47

*For the Chief Musician. A Psalm by the sons of Korah.*

<sup>1</sup> Oh clap your hands, all you nations.  
&emsp;Shout to God with the voice of triumph!  
<sup>2</sup> For the LORD Most High is awesome.  
&emsp;He is a great King over all the earth.  
<sup>3</sup> He subdues nations under us,  
&emsp;and peoples under our feet.  
<sup>4</sup> He chooses our inheritance for us,  
&emsp;the glory of Jacob whom he loved. *Selah.*  
<sup>5</sup> God has gone up with a shout,  
&emsp;the LORD with the sound of a trumpet.  
<sup>6</sup> Sing praises to God! Sing praises!  
&emsp;Sing praises to our King! Sing praises!  
<sup>7</sup> For God is the King of all the earth.  
&emsp;Sing praises with understanding.  
<sup>8</sup> God reigns over the nations.  
&emsp;God sits on his holy throne.  
<sup>9</sup> The princes of the peoples are gathered together,  
~~the people of the God of Abraham~~ <ins>with the people of the God of Abraham</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c173"></a>[c173](#c173)</sup>.  
&emsp;For the shields of the earth belong to God.  
&emsp;He is greatly exalted!

## Psalm 48

*A Song. A Psalm by the sons of Korah.*

<sup>1</sup> Great is the LORD, and greatly to be praised,  
&emsp;in the city of our God, in his holy mountain.  
<sup>2</sup> Beautiful in elevation, the joy of the whole earth,  
&emsp;is Mount Zion, on the north sides,  
&emsp;the city of the great King.  
<sup>3</sup> God has shown himself in her citadels as a refuge.  
<sup>4</sup> For, behold, the kings assembled themselves,  
&emsp;they passed by together.  
<sup>5</sup> They saw it, then they were amazed.  
&emsp;They were dismayed.  
&emsp;They hurried away.  
<sup>6</sup> Trembling took hold of them there,  
&emsp;pain, as of a woman in travail.  
<sup>7</sup> With the east wind, you break the ships of Tarshish.  
<sup>8</sup> As we have heard, so we have seen,  
&emsp;in the city of the LORD of Armies, in the city of our God.  
God will establish it forever. *Selah.*  
<sup>9</sup> We have thought about your loving kindness, God,  
&emsp;in the middle of your temple.  
<sup>10</sup> As is your name, God,  
&emsp;so is your praise to the ends of the earth.  
&emsp;Your right hand is full of righteousness.  
<sup>11</sup> Let Mount Zion be glad!  
&emsp;Let the daughters of Judah rejoice because of your judgments.  
<sup>12</sup> Walk about Zion, and go around her.  
&emsp;Number its towers.  
<sup>13</sup> Notice her bulwarks.  
&emsp;Consider her palaces,  
&emsp;that you may tell it to the next generation.  
<sup>14</sup> For this God is our God forever and ever.  
&emsp;He will be our guide even to death.

## Psalm 49

*For the Chief Musician. A Psalm by the sons of Korah.*

<sup>1</sup> Hear this, all you peoples.  
&emsp;Listen, all you inhabitants of the world,  
&emsp;<sup>2</sup> both low and high,  
&emsp;rich and poor together.  
<sup>3</sup> My mouth will speak words of wisdom.  
&emsp;My heart will utter understanding.  
<sup>4</sup> I will incline my ear to a proverb.  
&emsp;I will solve my riddle on the harp.  
<sup>5</sup> Why should I fear in the days of evil,  
&emsp;when iniquity at my heels surrounds me?  
<sup>6</sup> Those who trust in their ~~wealth~~ <ins>strength</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c174"></a>[c174](#c174)</sup>,  
&emsp;and boast in the multitude of their riches—  
&emsp;<sup>7</sup> none of them can by any means redeem his brother,  
&emsp;nor give God a ransom for him.  
<sup>8</sup> For the redemption of their life is costly,  
&emsp;no payment is ever enough,  
&emsp;<sup>9</sup> that he should live on forever,  
&emsp;that he should not see corruption.  
<sup>10</sup> For he sees that wise men die;  
&emsp;likewise the fool and the senseless perish,  
&emsp;and leave their wealth to others.  
<sup>11</sup> Their inward thought is that their houses will endure forever,  
&emsp;and their dwelling places to all generations.  
&emsp;They name their lands after themselves.  
<sup>12</sup> But man, despite his riches, doesn’t endure.  
&emsp;He is like the animals that perish.

<sup>13</sup> This is the destiny of those who are foolish,  
&emsp;and of those who approve their sayings. *Selah.*  
<sup>14</sup> They are appointed as a flock for Sheol.[^21]  
&emsp;Death shall ~~be their shepherd~~ <ins>feed on them</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c175"></a>[c175](#c175)</sup>.  
The upright shall have dominion over them in the morning.  
&emsp;Their beauty shall decay in Sheol,[^22]  
&emsp;far from their mansion.  
<sup>15</sup> But God will redeem my soul from the power of Sheol,[^23]  
&emsp;for he will receive me. *Selah.*  
<sup>16</sup> Don’t be afraid when a man is made rich,  
&emsp;when the glory of his house is increased;  
<sup>17</sup> for when he dies he will carry nothing away.  
&emsp;His glory won’t descend after him.  
<sup>18</sup> Though while he lived he blessed his soul—  
&emsp;and men praise you when you do well for yourself—  
&emsp;<sup>19</sup> he shall go to the generation of his fathers.  
&emsp;They shall never see the light.  
<sup>20</sup> A man who has riches without understanding,  
&emsp;is like the animals that perish.

## Psalm 50

*A Psalm by Asaph.*

<sup>1</sup> The Mighty One, God, the LORD, speaks,  
&emsp;and calls the earth from sunrise to sunset.  
<sup>2</sup> Out of Zion, the perfection of beauty,  
&emsp;God shines out.  
<sup>3</sup> Our God comes, and does not keep silent.  
&emsp;A fire devours before him.  
&emsp;It is very stormy around him.  
<sup>4</sup> He calls to the heavens above,  
&emsp;to the earth, that he may judge his people:  
<sup>5</sup> “Gather my saints together to me,  
&emsp;those who have made a covenant with me by sacrifice.”  
<sup>6</sup> The heavens shall declare his righteousness,  
&emsp;for God himself is judge. *Selah.*  
<sup>7</sup> “Hear, my people, and I will speak.  
&emsp;Israel, I will testify against you.  
I am God, your God.  
<sup>8</sup> I don’t rebuke you for your sacrifices.  
&emsp;Your burnt offerings are continually before me.  
<sup>9</sup> I have no need for a bull from your stall,  
&emsp;nor male goats from your pens.  
<sup>10</sup> For every animal of the forest is mine,  
&emsp;and the livestock on a thousand hills.  
<sup>11</sup> I know all the birds of the ~~mountains~~ <ins>sky</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c176"></a>[c176](#c176)</sup>.  
&emsp;The wild animals of the field are mine.  
<sup>12</sup> If I were hungry, I would not tell you,  
&emsp;for the world is mine, and all that is in it.  
<sup>13</sup> Will I eat the meat of bulls,  
&emsp;or drink the blood of goats?  
<sup>14</sup> Offer to God the sacrifice of thanksgiving.  
&emsp;Pay your vows to the Most High.  
<sup>15</sup> Call on me in the day of trouble.  
&emsp;I will deliver you, and you will honor me.”

<sup>16</sup> But to the wicked God says,  
&emsp;“What right do you have to declare my statutes,  
&emsp;that you have taken my covenant on your lips,  
&emsp;<sup>17</sup> since you hate instruction,  
&emsp;and throw my words behind you?  
<sup>18</sup> When you saw a thief, you ~~consented with him~~ <ins>ran with him</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c177"></a>[c177](#c177)</sup>,  
&emsp;and have participated with adulterers.

<sup>19</sup> “You give your mouth to evil.  
&emsp;Your tongue frames deceit.  
<sup>20</sup> You sit and speak against your brother.  
&emsp;You slander your own mother’s son.  
<sup>21</sup> You have done these things, and I kept silent.  
&emsp;You thought that I was just like you.  
&emsp;I will rebuke you, and accuse you in front of your eyes.

<sup>22</sup> “Now consider this, you who forget God,  
&emsp;lest I tear you into pieces, and there be no one to deliver.  
<sup>23</sup> Whoever offers the sacrifice of thanksgiving glorifies me,  
&emsp;and prepares his way so that I will show God’s salvation to him.”

## Psalm 51

*For the Chief Musician. A Psalm by David, when Nathan the prophet came to him, after he had gone in to Bathsheba.*

<sup>1</sup> Have mercy on me, God, according to your loving kindness.  
&emsp;According to the multitude of your tender mercies, blot out my transgressions.  
<sup>2</sup> Wash me thoroughly from my iniquity.  
&emsp;Cleanse me from my sin.  
<sup>3</sup> For I know my transgressions.  
&emsp;My sin is constantly before me.  
<sup>4</sup> Against you, and you only, I have sinned,  
&emsp;and done that which is evil in your sight,  
so you may be proved right when you speak,  
&emsp;and justified when you judge.  
<sup>5</sup> Behold, I was born in iniquity.  
&emsp;My mother conceived me in sin.  
<sup>6</sup> Behold, you ~~desire~~ <ins>have loved</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c178"></a>[c178](#c178)</sup> truth in the inward parts.  
&emsp;You teach me wisdom in ~~the inmost place~~ <ins>the hidden place</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c179"></a>[c179](#c179)</sup>.  
<sup>7</sup> Purify me with hyssop, and I will be clean.  
&emsp;Wash me, and I will be whiter than snow.  
<sup>8</sup> Let me hear joy and gladness,  
&emsp;that the bones which you have broken may rejoice.  
<sup>9</sup> Hide your face from my sins,  
&emsp;and blot out all of my iniquities.  
<sup>10</sup> Create in me a clean heart, O God.  
&emsp;Renew a right spirit ~~within me~~ <ins>within my innermost being</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c180"></a>[c180](#c180)</sup>.  
<sup>11</sup> Don’t throw me from your presence,  
&emsp;and don’t take your Holy Spirit from me.  
<sup>12</sup> Restore to me the joy of your salvation.  
&emsp;Uphold me with a willing spirit.  
<sup>13</sup> Then I will teach transgressors your ways.  
&emsp;Sinners will be converted to you.  
<sup>14</sup> Deliver me from the guilt of bloodshed, O God, the God of my salvation.  
&emsp;My tongue will sing aloud of your righteousness.  
<sup>15</sup> Lord, open my lips.  
&emsp;My mouth will declare your praise.  
<sup>16</sup> For you don’t delight in sacrifice, or else I would give it.  
&emsp;You have no pleasure in burnt offering.  
<sup>17</sup> The sacrifices of God are a broken spirit.  
&emsp;O God, you will not despise a broken and contrite heart.

<sup>18</sup> Do well in your good pleasure to Zion.  
&emsp;Build the walls of Jerusalem.  
<sup>19</sup> Then you will delight in the sacrifices of righteousness,  
&emsp;in burnt offerings and in whole burnt offerings.  
Then they will offer bulls on your altar.

## Psalm 52

*For the Chief Musician. A contemplation by David, when Doeg the Edomite came and told Saul, “David has come to Ahimelech’s house.”*

<sup>1</sup> Why do you boast of mischief, mighty man?  
&emsp;~~God’s loving kindness endures continually~~ <ins>All day you have devised plots</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c181"></a>[c181](#c181)</sup>.  
<sup>2</sup> Your tongue plots destruction,  
&emsp;like a sharp razor, working deceitfully.  
<sup>3</sup> You love evil more than good,  
&emsp;lying rather than speaking the truth. *Selah.*  
<sup>4</sup> You love all devouring words,  
&emsp;you deceitful tongue.  
<sup>5</sup> God will likewise destroy you forever.  
&emsp;He will take you up, and pluck you out of your tent,  
&emsp;and root you out of the land of the living. *Selah.*  
<sup>6</sup> The righteous also will see it, and fear,  
&emsp;and laugh at him, saying,  
<sup>7</sup> “Behold, this is the man who didn’t make God his strength,  
&emsp;but trusted in the abundance of his riches,  
&emsp;~~and strengthened himself in his wickedness.~~ <ins>and prevailed in his treachery.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c182"></a>[c182](#c182)</sup>”  
<sup>8</sup> But as for me, I am like a green olive tree in God’s house.  
&emsp;I trust in God’s loving kindness forever and ever.  
<sup>9</sup> I will give you thanks forever, because you have done it.  
&emsp;I will hope in your name, for it is good,  
&emsp;in the presence of your saints.

## Psalm 53

*For the Chief Musician. To the tune of “Mahalath.” A contemplation by David.*

<sup>1</sup> The fool has said in his heart, “There is no God.”  
&emsp;They are corrupt, and have done abominable iniquity.  
&emsp;There is no one who does good.  
<sup>2</sup> God looks down from heaven on the children of men,  
&emsp;to see if there are any who understood,  
&emsp;who seek after God.  
<sup>3</sup> Every one of them has gone back.  
&emsp;They have become filthy together.  
&emsp;There is no one who does good, no, not one.  
<sup>4</sup> Have the workers of iniquity no knowledge,  
&emsp;who eat up my people as they eat bread,  
&emsp;and don’t call on God?  
<sup>5</sup> There they were in great fear, where no fear was,  
&emsp;for God has scattered the bones of him who encamps against you.  
~~You have put them to shame~~ <ins>They were put to shame</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c183"></a>[c183](#c183)</sup>,  
&emsp;because God has rejected them.  
<sup>6</sup> Oh that the salvation of Israel would come out of Zion!  
&emsp;When God brings back his people from captivity,  
&emsp;then Jacob shall rejoice,  
&emsp;and Israel shall be glad.

## Psalm 54

*For the Chief Musician. On stringed instruments. A contemplation by David, when the Ziphites came and said to Saul, “Isn’t David hiding himself among us?”*

<sup>1</sup> Save me, God, by your name.  
&emsp;Vindicate me in your might.  
<sup>2</sup> Hear my prayer, God.  
&emsp;Listen to the words of my mouth.  
<sup>3</sup> For strangers have risen up against me.  
&emsp;~~Violent men~~ <ins>The mighty</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c184"></a>[c184](#c184)</sup> have sought after my soul.  
&emsp;They haven’t set God before them. *Selah.*  
<sup>4</sup> Behold, God is my helper.  
&emsp;The Lord is the one who sustains my soul.  
<sup>5</sup> He will repay the evil to my enemies.  
&emsp;Destroy them in your truth.  
<sup>6</sup> With a free will offering, I will sacrifice to you.  
&emsp;I will give thanks to your name, LORD, for it is good.  
<sup>7</sup> For he has delivered me out of all trouble.  
&emsp;My eye has seen triumph over my enemies.

## Psalm 55

*For the Chief Musician. On stringed instruments. A contemplation by David.*

<sup>1</sup> Listen to my prayer, God.  
&emsp;Don’t hide yourself from my supplication.  
<sup>2</sup> Attend to me, and answer me.  
&emsp;I am restless in my ~~complaint~~ <ins>meditation</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c185"></a>[c185](#c185)</sup>,  
&emsp;and ~~moan~~ <ins>am disturbed</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c186"></a>[c186](#c186)</sup> <sup>3</sup> because of the voice of the enemy,  
&emsp;because of the oppression of the wicked.  
For they bring ~~suffering~~ <ins>iniquity</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c187"></a>[c187](#c187)</sup> on me.  
&emsp;In anger they hold a grudge against me.  
<sup>4</sup> My heart is severely pained within me.  
&emsp;The terrors of death have fallen on me.  
<sup>5</sup> Fearfulness and trembling have come on me.  
&emsp;Horror has overwhelmed me.  
<sup>6</sup> I said, “~~Oh that I had wings like a dove!~~ <ins>Oh, who will give me wings like a dove?</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c188"></a>[c188](#c188)</sup>  
&emsp;~~Then I would fly away, and be at rest.~~ <ins>Then I will fly away, and be at rest.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c189"></a>[c189](#c189)</sup>  
<sup>7</sup> Behold, ~~then I would wander far off.~~ <ins>then I will flee far away.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c190"></a>[c190](#c190)</sup>  
&emsp;~~I would lodge in the wilderness.~~ <ins>I will lodge in the wilderness.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c191"></a>[c191](#c191)</sup>” *Selah.*  
<sup>8</sup> “~~I would hurry to a shelter~~ <ins>I will wait for him who saves me</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c192"></a>[c192](#c192)</sup> from the stormy wind and storm.”  
<sup>9</sup> Confuse them, Lord, and confound their language,  
&emsp;for I have seen violence and strife in the city.  
<sup>10</sup> Day and night they prowl around on its walls.  
&emsp;Malice and abuse are also within her.  
<sup>11</sup> Destructive forces are within her.  
&emsp;Threats and lies don’t depart from her streets.  
<sup>12</sup> For it was not an enemy who insulted me,  
&emsp;then I could have endured it.  
Neither was it he who hated me who raised himself up against me,  
&emsp;then I would have hidden myself from him.  
<sup>13</sup> But it was you, a man like me,  
&emsp;my companion, and my familiar friend.  
<sup>14</sup> We took sweet fellowship together.  
&emsp;We walked in God’s house with company.  
<sup>15</sup> Let death come suddenly on them.  
&emsp;Let them go down alive into Sheol.[^24]  
&emsp;For wickedness is among them, in their dwelling.  
<sup>16</sup> As for me, I will call on God.  
&emsp;The LORD will save me.  
<sup>17</sup> Evening, morning, and at noon, I will ~~cry out in distress~~ <ins>ponder and moan</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c193"></a>[c193](#c193)</sup>.  
&emsp;He will hear my voice.  
<sup>18</sup> He has redeemed my soul in peace from the battle that was against me,  
&emsp;although there are many who oppose me.  
<sup>19</sup> God, who is enthroned forever,  
&emsp;will hear and answer them. *Selah.*

They never change  
&emsp;and don’t fear God.  
<sup>20</sup> He raises his hands against his friends.  
&emsp;He has violated his covenant.  
<sup>21</sup> His mouth was smooth as butter,  
&emsp;but his heart was war.  
His words were softer than oil,  
&emsp;yet they were drawn swords.

<sup>22</sup> Cast your burden on the LORD and he will sustain you.  
&emsp;He will never allow the righteous to be moved.  
<sup>23</sup> But you, God, will bring them down into the pit of destruction.  
&emsp;Bloodthirsty and deceitful men shall not live out half their days,  
&emsp;but I will trust in you.

## Psalm 56

*For the Chief Musician. To the tune of “Silent Dove in Distant Lands.” A poem by David, when the Philistines seized him in Gath.*

<sup>1</sup> Be merciful to me, God, for man wants to swallow me up.  
&emsp;All day long, he attacks and oppresses me.  
<sup>2</sup> My enemies want to swallow me up all day long,  
&emsp;for they are many who fight ~~proudly against me~~ <ins>against me, O Most High</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c194"></a>[c194](#c194)</sup>.  
<sup>3</sup> When I am afraid,  
&emsp;I will put my trust in you.  
<sup>4</sup> In God, I praise his word.  
&emsp;In God, I put my trust.  
I will not be afraid.  
&emsp;What can flesh do to me?  
<sup>5</sup> All day long they twist my words.  
&emsp;All their thoughts are against me for evil.  
<sup>6</sup> They conspire and lurk,  
&emsp;watching my steps.  
&emsp;They are eager to take my life.  
<sup>7</sup> ~~Shall they escape by iniquity?~~ <ins>Repay them for their iniquity.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c195"></a>[c195](#c195)</sup>  
&emsp;In anger cast down the peoples, God.  
<sup>8</sup> You count my wanderings.  
&emsp;You put my tears into your container.  
&emsp;Aren’t they in your book?  
<sup>9</sup> Then my enemies shall turn back in the day that I call.  
&emsp;I know this: that God is for me.  
<sup>10</sup> In God, I will praise his word.  
&emsp;In the LORD, I will praise his word.  
<sup>11</sup> I have put my trust in God.  
&emsp;I will not be afraid.  
&emsp;What can man do to me?  
<sup>12</sup> Your vows are on me, God.  
&emsp;I will give thank offerings to you.  
<sup>13</sup> For you have delivered my soul from death,  
&emsp;and prevented my feet from falling,  
&emsp;that I may walk before God in the light of the living.

## Psalm 57

*For the Chief Musician. To the tune of “Do Not Destroy.” A poem by David, when he fled from Saul, in the cave.*

<sup>1</sup> Be merciful to me, God, be merciful to me,  
&emsp;for my soul takes refuge in you.  
Yes, in the shadow of your wings, I will take refuge,  
&emsp;until ~~disaster has passed~~ <ins>the plots have passed</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c196"></a>[c196](#c196)</sup>.  
<sup>2</sup> I cry out to God Most High,  
to God who ~~accomplishes my requests for me~~ <ins>has done good to me</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c197"></a>[c197](#c197)</sup>.  
<sup>3</sup> He will send from heaven, and save me,  
&emsp;he rebukes the one who is pursuing me. *Selah.*  
God will send out his loving kindness and his truth.  
<sup>4</sup> My soul is among ~~lions~~ <ins>lion cubs</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c198"></a>[c198](#c198)</sup>.  
&emsp;I lie among those ~~who are set on fire~~ <ins>who devour</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c199"></a>[c199](#c199)</sup>,  
&emsp;even the sons of men, whose teeth are spears and arrows,  
&emsp;and their tongue a sharp sword.  
<sup>5</sup> Be exalted, God, above the heavens!  
&emsp;Let your glory be above all the earth!

<sup>6</sup> They have prepared a net for my steps.  
&emsp;My soul is bowed down.  
They dig a pit before me.  
&emsp;They fall into the middle of it themselves. *Selah.*  
<sup>7</sup> My heart is steadfast, God.  
&emsp;My heart is steadfast.  
&emsp;I will sing, yes, I will sing praises.  
<sup>8</sup> Wake up, my glory! Wake up, lute and harp!  
&emsp;I will wake up the dawn.  
<sup>9</sup> I will give thanks to you, Lord, among the peoples.  
&emsp;I will sing ~~praises~~ <ins>a psalm</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c200"></a>[c200](#c200)</sup> to you among the nations.  
<sup>10</sup> For your great loving kindness reaches to the heavens,  
&emsp;and your truth to the skies.  
<sup>11</sup> Be exalted, God, above the heavens.  
&emsp;Let your glory be over all the earth.

## Psalm 58

*For the Chief Musician. To the tune of “Do Not Destroy.” A poem by David.*

<sup>1</sup> Do you indeed speak righteousness, silent ones?  
&emsp;Do you judge blamelessly, you sons of men?  
<sup>2</sup> No, in your heart you plot injustice.  
&emsp;You measure out the violence of your hands on the earth.  
<sup>3</sup> The wicked go astray from the womb.  
&emsp;They are wayward as soon as they are born, speaking lies.  
<sup>4</sup> Their poison is like the poison of a snake,  
&emsp;like a deaf cobra that stops its ear,  
&emsp;<sup>5</sup> which doesn’t listen to the voice of charmers,  
&emsp;no matter how skillful the charmer may be.  
<sup>6</sup> Break their teeth, God, in their mouth.  
&emsp;Break out the great teeth of the young lions, LORD.  
<sup>7</sup> Let them vanish like water that flows away.  
&emsp;~~When they draw the bow, let their arrows be made blunt~~ <ins>Like trampled grass, let them wither</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c201"></a>[c201](#c201)</sup>.  
<sup>8</sup> Let them be like a snail which melts and passes away,  
&emsp;like the stillborn child, who has not seen the sun.  
<sup>9</sup> Before your pots can feel the heat of the thorns,  
&emsp;~~he will sweep away~~ <ins>the heat of wrath will devour</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c202"></a>[c202](#c202)</sup> the green and the burning alike.  
<sup>10</sup> The righteous shall rejoice when he sees the vengeance.  
&emsp;He shall wash his feet in the blood of the wicked,  
<sup>11</sup> so that men shall say, “Most certainly there is a reward for the righteous.  
&emsp;Most certainly there is a God who judges the earth.”

## Psalm 59

*For the Chief Musician. To the tune of “Do Not Destroy.” A poem by David, when Saul sent, and they watched the house to kill him.*

<sup>1</sup> Deliver me from my enemies, my God.  
&emsp;Set me on high from those who rise up against me.  
<sup>2</sup> Deliver me from the workers of iniquity.  
&emsp;Save me from the bloodthirsty men.  
<sup>3</sup> For, behold, they lie in wait for my soul.  
&emsp;The mighty ~~gather themselves together against me~~ <ins>have rushed upon me</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c203"></a>[c203](#c203)</sup>,  
&emsp;not for my disobedience, nor for my sin, LORD.  
<sup>4</sup> I have done no wrong, yet they are ready to attack me.  
&emsp;Rise up, behold, and help me!  
<sup>5</sup> You, LORD God of Armies, the God of Israel,  
&emsp;rouse yourself to punish the nations.  
&emsp;Show no mercy to the wicked traitors. *Selah.*  
<sup>6</sup> They return at evening, howling like dogs,  
&emsp;and prowl around the city.  
<sup>7</sup> Behold, they spew with their mouth.  
&emsp;Swords are in their lips,  
&emsp;“For”, they say, “who hears us?”  
<sup>8</sup> But you, LORD, laugh at them.  
&emsp;You scoff at all the nations.  
<sup>9</sup> Oh, my Strength, I watch for you,  
&emsp;for God is my high tower.  
<sup>10</sup> My God will go before me with his loving kindness.  
&emsp;God will let me look at my enemies in triumph.  
<sup>11</sup> Don’t kill them, or my people may forget.  
&emsp;Scatter them by your power, and bring them down, Lord our shield.  
<sup>12</sup> For the sin of their mouth, and the words of their lips,  
&emsp;let them be caught in their pride,  
&emsp;for the curses and lies which they utter.  
<sup>13</sup> Consume them in wrath.  
&emsp;Consume them, and they will be no more.  
Let them know that God rules in Jacob,  
&emsp;to the ends of the earth. *Selah.*  
<sup>14</sup> At evening let them return.  
&emsp;Let them howl like a dog, and go around the city.  
<sup>15</sup> They shall wander up and down for food,  
&emsp;and wait all night if they aren’t satisfied.

<sup>16</sup> But I will sing of your strength.  
&emsp;Yes, I will ~~sing aloud of~~ <ins>rejoice in</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c204"></a>[c204](#c204)</sup> your loving kindness in the morning.  
For you have been my high tower,  
&emsp;a refuge in the day of my distress.  
<sup>17</sup> To you, my strength, I will sing praises.  
&emsp;For God is my high tower, the God of my mercy.

## Psalm 60

*For the Chief Musician. To the tune of “The Lily of the Covenant.” A teaching poem by David, when he fought with Aram Naharaim and with Aram Zobah, and Joab returned, and killed twelve thousand of Edom in the Valley of Salt.*

<sup>1</sup> God, you have rejected us.  
&emsp;You have broken us down.  
You have been angry.  
&emsp;~~Restore us~~ <ins>Turn back to us</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c205"></a>[c205](#c205)</sup>, again.  
<sup>2</sup> You have made the land tremble.  
&emsp;You have torn it.  
Mend its fractures,  
&emsp;for it quakes.  
<sup>3</sup> You have shown your people hard things.  
&emsp;You have made us drink the wine that makes us stagger.  
<sup>4</sup> You have given a banner to those who fear you,  
&emsp;that it may be displayed because of the truth. *Selah.*

<sup>5</sup> So that your beloved may be delivered,  
&emsp;save with your right hand, and answer us.  
<sup>6</sup> God has spoken from his sanctuary:  
&emsp;“I will triumph.  
&emsp;I will divide Shechem,  
&emsp;and measure out the valley of Succoth.  
<sup>7</sup> Gilead is mine, and Manasseh is mine.  
&emsp;Ephraim also is the defense of my head.  
&emsp;Judah is my scepter.  
<sup>8</sup> Moab is my wash basin.  
&emsp;I will ~~throw~~ <ins>stretch out</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c206"></a>[c206](#c206)</sup> my sandal on Edom.  
&emsp;I shout in triumph over Philistia.”

<sup>9</sup> Who will bring me into the strong city?  
&emsp;Who has led me to Edom?  
<sup>10</sup> Haven’t you, God, rejected us?  
&emsp;You don’t go out with our armies, God.  
<sup>11</sup> Give us help ~~against the adversary~~ <ins>from trouble</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c207"></a>[c207](#c207)</sup>,  
&emsp;for the help of man is vain.  
<sup>12</sup> Through God we will do valiantly,  
&emsp;for it is he who will tread down our adversaries.

## Psalm 61

*For the Chief Musician. For a stringed instrument. By David.*

<sup>1</sup> Hear ~~my cry~~ <ins>my supplication</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c208"></a>[c208](#c208)</sup>, God.  
&emsp;Listen to my prayer.  
<sup>2</sup> From the end of the earth, I ~~will call~~ <ins>have cried</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c209"></a>[c209](#c209)</sup> to you when my heart ~~is overwhelmed~~ <ins>was distressed</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c211"></a>[c211](#c211)</sup>.  
&emsp;Lead me to the rock that is ~~higher than I~~ <ins>inaccessible to me</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c210"></a>[c210](#c210)</sup>.  
<sup>3</sup> For you have been ~~a refuge for me~~ <ins>my hope</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c212"></a>[c212](#c212)</sup>,  
&emsp;a strong tower from the enemy.  
<sup>4</sup> I will dwell in your tent forever.  
&emsp;I will take refuge in the shelter of your wings. *Selah.*  
<sup>5</sup> For you, God, have heard my vows.  
&emsp;You have given me the heritage of those who fear your name.  
<sup>6</sup> You will prolong the king’s life.  
&emsp;His years will be for generations.  
<sup>7</sup> He shall ~~be enthroned~~ <ins>sit</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c213"></a>[c213](#c213)</sup> in God’s presence forever.  
&emsp;Appoint your loving kindness and truth, that they may preserve him.  
<sup>8</sup> So I will ~~sing praise~~ <ins>sing a psalm</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c214"></a>[c214](#c214)</sup> to your name forever,  
&emsp;that I may fulfill my vows daily.

## Psalm 62

*For the Chief Musician. To Jeduthun. A Psalm by David.*

<sup>1</sup> My soul rests in God alone.  
&emsp;My salvation is from him.  
<sup>2</sup> He alone is my ~~rock~~ <ins>refuge</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c215"></a>[c215](#c215)</sup>, my salvation, and my fortress.  
&emsp;I will never be greatly shaken.  
<sup>3</sup> How long will you assault a man?  
&emsp;Would all of you throw him down,  
&emsp;like a leaning wall, like a tottering fence?  
<sup>4</sup> They fully intend to throw him down from his lofty place.  
&emsp;They delight in lies.  
&emsp;They bless with their mouth, but they curse ~~inwardly~~ <ins>in their heart</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c216"></a>[c216](#c216)</sup>. *Selah.*  
<sup>5</sup> My soul, ~~wait in silence for God alone~~ <ins>be at rest in God alone</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c217"></a>[c217](#c217)</sup>,  
&emsp;for my ~~expectation~~ <ins>patience</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c218"></a>[c218](#c218)</sup> is from him.  
<sup>6</sup> He alone is my rock and my salvation, my fortress.  
&emsp;I will not be shaken.  
<sup>7</sup> My salvation and my honor is with God.  
&emsp;~~The rock of my strength, and my refuge, is in God.~~ <ins>He is the God of my strength, and my refuge is in God.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c219"></a>[c219](#c219)</sup>  
<sup>8</sup> Trust in him at all times, you people.  
&emsp;Pour out your heart before him.  
&emsp;God is a refuge for us. *Selah.*  
<sup>9</sup> Surely ~~men of low degree~~ <ins>the sons of Adam</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c221"></a>[c221](#c221)</sup> are just ~~a breath~~ <ins>vanity</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c220"></a>[c220](#c220)</sup>,  
&emsp;and ~~men of high degree~~ <ins>the sons of men</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c222"></a>[c222](#c222)</sup> are a lie.  
In the balances they will go up.  
&emsp;They are together lighter than ~~a breath~~ <ins>smoke</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c223"></a>[c223](#c223)</sup>.  
<sup>10</sup> Don’t trust in ~~oppression~~ <ins>violence</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c224"></a>[c224](#c224)</sup>.  
&emsp;Don’t become vain in robbery.  
If riches increase,  
&emsp;don’t set your heart on them.  
<sup>11</sup> God has spoken once;  
&emsp;twice I have heard this,  
&emsp;that power belongs to God.  
<sup>12</sup> Also to you, Lord, belongs loving kindness,  
&emsp;for you reward every man according to his work.

## Psalm 63

*A Psalm by David, when he was in the desert of Judah.*

<sup>1</sup> God, you are my God.  
&emsp;I will ~~earnestly seek you~~ <ins>watch for you at dawn</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c225"></a>[c225](#c225)</sup>.  
My soul thirsts for you.  
&emsp;My flesh longs for you,  
&emsp;in a dry and ~~weary~~ <ins>desert</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c226"></a>[c226](#c226)</sup> land, where there is no water.  
<sup>2</sup> So ~~I have seen you in the sanctuary~~ <ins>I have appeared before you in the sanctuary</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c227"></a>[c227](#c227)</sup>,  
&emsp;watching your power and your glory.  
<sup>3</sup> Because your loving kindness is better than life,  
&emsp;my lips shall praise you.  
<sup>4</sup> So I will bless you while I live.  
&emsp;I will lift up my hands in your name.  
<sup>5</sup> My soul shall be satisfied as with the richest food.  
&emsp;My mouth shall praise you with joyful lips,  
&emsp;<sup>6</sup> when I remember you on my bed,  
&emsp;and think about you ~~in the night watches~~ <ins>in the morning</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c228"></a>[c228](#c228)</sup>.  
<sup>7</sup> For you have been my help.  
&emsp;I will rejoice in the shadow of your wings.  
<sup>8</sup> My soul stays close to you.  
&emsp;Your right hand holds me up.  
<sup>9</sup> But those who seek my soul to destroy it  
&emsp;shall go into the lower parts of the earth.  
<sup>10</sup> They shall be given over to the power of the sword.  
&emsp;They shall be jackal food.  
<sup>11</sup> But the king shall rejoice in God.  
&emsp;Everyone who swears by him will praise him,  
&emsp;for the mouth of those who ~~speak lies~~ <ins>speak iniquity</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c229"></a>[c229](#c229)</sup> shall be silenced.

## Psalm 64

*For the Chief Musician. A Psalm by David.*

<sup>1</sup> Hear my voice, God, in my ~~complaint~~ <ins>meditation</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c230"></a>[c230](#c230)</sup>.  
&emsp;Preserve my life from fear of the enemy.  
<sup>2</sup> ~~Hide~~ <ins>Protect</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c231"></a>[c231](#c231)</sup> me from the ~~conspiracy~~ <ins>gathering</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c232"></a>[c232](#c232)</sup> of the wicked,  
&emsp;from the noisy crowd of the ones doing evil;  
<sup>3</sup> who sharpen their tongue like a sword,  
&emsp;and aim their arrows, deadly words,  
&emsp;<sup>4</sup> to shoot innocent men from ambushes.  
&emsp;They shoot at him suddenly and fearlessly.  
<sup>5</sup> They encourage themselves in evil plans.  
&emsp;They talk about laying snares secretly.  
&emsp;They say, “Who will see them?”  
<sup>6</sup> ~~They plot injustice, saying, “We have made a perfect plan!”~~ <ins>They devised wickedness; they completed the plans they had devised.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c234"></a>[c234](#c234)</sup>  
&emsp;~~Surely man’s mind and heart are cunning.~~ <ins>A man’s inward parts and heart are an abyss.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c233"></a>[c233](#c233)</sup>  
<sup>7</sup> But God will shoot at them.  
&emsp;They will be suddenly struck down with an arrow.  
<sup>8</sup> Their own tongues shall ~~ruin them~~ <ins>weaken them</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c235"></a>[c235](#c235)</sup>.  
&emsp;All who see them will shake their heads.  
<sup>9</sup> All mankind shall be afraid.  
&emsp;They shall declare the work of God,  
&emsp;and shall wisely ponder what he has done.  
<sup>10</sup> The righteous shall be glad in the LORD,  
&emsp;and shall take refuge in him.  
&emsp;All the upright in heart shall praise him!

## Psalm 65

*For the Chief Musician. A Psalm by David. A song.*

<sup>1</sup> Praise waits for you, God, in Zion.  
&emsp;Vows shall be ~~performed to you~~ <ins>performed to you in Jerusalem</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c236"></a>[c236](#c236)</sup>.  
<sup>2</sup> You who hear prayer,  
&emsp;all men will come to you.  
<sup>3</sup> Sins overwhelmed me,  
&emsp;but you atoned for our transgressions.  
<sup>4</sup> Blessed is the one whom you choose and cause to come near,  
&emsp;that he may live in your courts.  
&emsp;We will be filled with the goodness of your house,  
&emsp;your holy temple.  
<sup>5</sup> By awesome deeds of righteousness, you answer us,  
&emsp;God of our salvation.  
You who are the hope of all the ends of the earth,  
&emsp;of those who are far away on the sea.  
<sup>6</sup> By your power, you form the mountains,  
&emsp;having armed yourself with strength.  
<sup>7</sup> You still the roaring of the seas,  
&emsp;the roaring of their waves,  
&emsp;and the turmoil of the nations.  
<sup>8</sup> They also who dwell in faraway places are afraid at your wonders.  
&emsp;You call the morning’s dawn and the evening with songs of joy.  
<sup>9</sup> You visit the earth, and water it.  
&emsp;You greatly enrich it.  
The river of God is full of water.  
&emsp;You provide them grain, for so you have ordained it.  
<sup>10</sup> You drench its furrows.  
&emsp;You level its ridges.  
&emsp;You soften it with showers.  
&emsp;You bless it with a crop.  
<sup>11</sup> You crown the year with your bounty.  
&emsp;Your ~~carts~~ <ins>footsteps</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c237"></a>[c237](#c237)</sup> overflow with abundance.  
<sup>12</sup> The wilderness grasslands overflow.  
&emsp;The hills are clothed with gladness.  
<sup>13</sup> The pastures are covered with flocks.  
&emsp;The valleys also are clothed with grain.  
They shout for joy!  
&emsp;They also sing.

## Psalm 66

*For the Chief Musician. A song. A Psalm.*

<sup>1</sup> Make a joyful shout to God, all the earth!  
&emsp;<sup>2</sup> Sing to the glory of his name!  
&emsp;Offer glory and praise!  
<sup>3</sup> Tell God, “How awesome are your deeds!  
&emsp;Through the greatness of your power, your enemies ~~submit themselves to you~~ <ins>fawn on you</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c238"></a>[c238](#c238)</sup>.  
<sup>4</sup> All the earth will worship you,  
&emsp;and will sing to you;  
&emsp;they will sing to your name.” *Selah.*  
<sup>5</sup> Come, and see God’s deeds—  
&emsp;awesome work on behalf of the children of men.  
<sup>6</sup> He turned the sea into dry land.  
&emsp;They went through the river on foot.  
&emsp;There, we rejoiced in him.  
<sup>7</sup> He rules by his might forever.  
&emsp;His eyes watch the nations.  
&emsp;Don’t let the rebellious rise up against him. *Selah.*  
<sup>8</sup> Praise our God, you peoples!  
&emsp;Make the sound of his praise heard,  
<sup>9</sup> who preserves our life among the living,  
&emsp;and doesn’t allow our feet to be moved.  
<sup>10</sup> For you, God, have tested us.  
&emsp;You have refined us, as silver is refined.  
<sup>11</sup> You brought us into prison.  
&emsp;You laid a burden on our backs.  
<sup>12</sup> You ~~allowed men to ride~~ <ins>put men</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c239"></a>[c239](#c239)</sup> over our heads.  
&emsp;We went through fire and through water,  
&emsp;but you brought us to the place of abundance.  
<sup>13</sup> I will come into your temple with burnt offerings.  
&emsp;I will pay my vows to you, <sup>14</sup> which my lips promised,  
&emsp;and my mouth spoke, when I was in distress.  
<sup>15</sup> I will offer to you burnt offerings of fat animals,  
&emsp;with the offering of rams,  
&emsp;I will offer bulls with goats. *Selah.*  
<sup>16</sup> Come and hear, all you who fear God.  
&emsp;I will declare what he has done for my soul.  
<sup>17</sup> I cried to him with my mouth.  
&emsp;He was extolled with my tongue.  
<sup>18</sup> If I cherished sin in my heart,  
&emsp;the Lord wouldn’t have listened.  
<sup>19</sup> But most certainly, God has listened.  
&emsp;He has heard the voice of my prayer.  
<sup>20</sup> Blessed be God, who has not turned away my prayer,  
&emsp;nor his loving kindness from me.

## Psalm 67

*For the Chief Musician. With stringed instruments. A Psalm. A song.*

<sup>1</sup> May God be merciful to us, bless us,  
&emsp;and cause his face to shine on us. *Selah.*  
<sup>2</sup> That your way may be known on earth,  
&emsp;and your salvation among all nations,  
<sup>3</sup> let the peoples praise you, God.  
&emsp;Let all the peoples ~~praise~~ <ins>confess</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c240"></a>[c240](#c240)</sup> you.  
<sup>4</sup> Oh let the nations be glad and sing for joy,  
&emsp;for you will judge the peoples with equity,  
&emsp;and govern the nations on earth. *Selah.*  
<sup>5</sup> Let the peoples praise you, God.  
&emsp;Let all the peoples praise you.  
<sup>6</sup> The earth has yielded its increase.  
&emsp;God, even our own God, will bless us.  
<sup>7</sup> God will bless us.  
&emsp;All the ends of the earth shall fear him.

## Psalm 68

*For the Chief Musician. A Psalm by David. A song.*

<sup>1</sup> Let God arise!  
&emsp;Let his enemies be scattered!  
&emsp;Let them who hate him also flee before him.  
<sup>2</sup> As smoke is driven away,  
&emsp;so drive them away.  
As wax melts before the fire,  
&emsp;so let the wicked perish at the presence of God.  
<sup>3</sup> But let the righteous be glad.  
&emsp;Let them rejoice before God.  
&emsp;Yes, let them rejoice with gladness.  
<sup>4</sup> Sing to God! Sing praises to his name!  
&emsp;~~Extol~~ <ins>Make a path for</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c241"></a>[c241](#c241)</sup> him who rides on the clouds:  
to the LORD,[^25] his name!  
&emsp;Rejoice before him!  
<sup>5</sup> A father of the fatherless, and a ~~defender~~ <ins>judge</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c242"></a>[c242](#c242)</sup> of the widows,  
&emsp;is God in his holy habitation.  
<sup>6</sup> God sets the lonely in families.  
He brings out the prisoners with singing,  
&emsp;but the rebellious dwell in a sun-scorched land.

<sup>7</sup> God, when you went out before your people,  
&emsp;when you marched through the wilderness... *Selah.*  
<sup>8</sup> The earth trembled.  
&emsp;The sky also poured down rain at the presence of the God of Sinai—  
&emsp;at the presence of God, the God of Israel.  
<sup>9</sup> You, God, sent a plentiful rain.  
&emsp;You confirmed your inheritance when it was weary.  
<sup>10</sup> Your congregation lived therein.  
&emsp;You, God, prepared your goodness for the poor.  
<sup>11</sup> The Lord announced the word.  
&emsp;The ones who proclaim it are a great company.  
<sup>12</sup> “Kings of armies flee! They flee!”  
&emsp;She who waits at home divides the plunder,  
&emsp;<sup>13</sup> while you sleep among the camp fires,  
&emsp;the wings of a dove sheathed with silver,  
&emsp;her feathers with shining gold.  
<sup>14</sup> When the Almighty scattered kings in her,  
&emsp;it snowed on Zalmon.  
<sup>15</sup> The mountains of Bashan are majestic mountains.  
&emsp;The mountains of Bashan are rugged.  
<sup>16</sup> Why do you look in envy, you rugged mountains,  
&emsp;at the mountain where God chooses to reign?  
&emsp;Yes, the LORD[^26] will dwell there forever.  
<sup>17</sup> The chariots of God are tens of thousands and thousands of thousands.  
&emsp;The Lord ~~is among them, from Sinai, into~~ <ins>comes from Sinai into</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c243"></a>[c243](#c243)</sup> the sanctuary.  
<sup>18</sup> You have ascended on high.  
&emsp;You have led away captives.  
You have received ~~gifts among people~~ <ins>people as gifts</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c244"></a>[c244](#c244)</sup>,  
&emsp;~~yes, among the rebellious also, that the LORD~~ <ins>that even the rebellious may dwell with the LORD</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c245"></a>[c245](#c245)</sup>[^27] ~~God might dwell there.~~ <ins>God.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c246"></a>[c246](#c246)</sup>

<sup>19</sup> Blessed be the Lord, who daily bears our burdens,  
&emsp;even the God who is our salvation. *Selah.*  
<sup>20</sup> God is to us a God of deliverance.  
&emsp;To GOD,[^28] the Lord, belongs escape from death.  
<sup>21</sup> But God will strike through the head of his enemies,  
&emsp;the hairy scalp of such a one as still continues in his guiltiness.  
<sup>22</sup> The Lord said, “I will bring you again from Bashan,  
&emsp;I will bring you again from the depths of the sea,  
<sup>23</sup> that you may crush them, dipping your foot in blood,  
&emsp;that the tongues of your dogs may have their portion from your enemies.”  
<sup>24</sup> They have seen your processions, God,  
&emsp;even the processions of my God, my King, into the sanctuary.  
<sup>25</sup> The singers went before, the minstrels followed after,  
&emsp;among the ladies playing with tambourines,  
<sup>26</sup> “Bless God in the congregations,  
&emsp;even the Lord in the assembly of Israel!”  
<sup>27</sup> There is little Benjamin, their ruler,  
&emsp;the princes of Judah, their council,  
&emsp;the princes of Zebulun, and the princes of Naphtali.

<sup>28</sup> Your God has commanded your strength.  
&emsp;Strengthen, God, that which you have done ~~for us~~ <ins>in us</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c247"></a>[c247](#c247)</sup>.  
<sup>29</sup> ~~Because of your temple~~ <ins>From your temple</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c248"></a>[c248](#c248)</sup> at Jerusalem,  
&emsp;kings will bring presents to you.  
<sup>30</sup> Rebuke the wild animal of the reeds,  
&emsp;the multitude of the bulls with the calves of the peoples.  
~~Trample under foot the bars of silver~~ <ins>Let them prostrate themselves with bars of silver</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c249"></a>[c249](#c249)</sup>.  
&emsp;Scatter the nations who delight in war.  
<sup>31</sup> Princes shall come out of Egypt.  
&emsp;Ethiopia shall hurry to stretch out her hands to God.  
<sup>32</sup> Sing to God, you kingdoms of the earth!  
&emsp;Sing praises to the Lord—*Selah—*  
<sup>33</sup> to him who rides on the heaven of ~~heavens, which are of old~~ <ins>heavens to the east</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c250"></a>[c250](#c250)</sup>;  
&emsp;behold, he utters his voice, a mighty voice.  
<sup>34</sup> Ascribe strength to God!  
&emsp;His excellency is over Israel,  
&emsp;his strength is in the skies.  
<sup>35</sup> You are awesome, God, in your sanctuaries.  
&emsp;The God of Israel gives strength and power to his people.  
&emsp;Praise be to God!

## Psalm 69

*For the Chief Musician. To the tune of “Lilies.” By David.*

<sup>1</sup> Save me, God,  
&emsp;for the waters have come up to my neck!  
<sup>2</sup> I sink in deep mire, where there is no foothold.  
&emsp;I have come into deep waters, where the floods overflow me.  
<sup>3</sup> I am weary with my crying.  
&emsp;My throat is dry.  
&emsp;My eyes fail looking for my God.  
<sup>4</sup> Those who hate me without a cause are more than the hairs of my head.  
&emsp;Those who want to cut me off, being my enemies wrongfully, are mighty.  
&emsp;I have to restore what I didn’t take away.  
<sup>5</sup> God, you know my foolishness.  
&emsp;My sins aren’t hidden from you.  
<sup>6</sup> Don’t let those who wait for you be shamed through me, Lord GOD of Armies.  
&emsp;Don’t let those who seek you be brought to dishonor through me, God of Israel.  
<sup>7</sup> Because for your sake, I have borne reproach.  
&emsp;Shame has covered my face.  
<sup>8</sup> I have become a stranger to my brothers,  
&emsp;an alien to my mother’s children.  
<sup>9</sup> For the zeal of your house consumes me.  
&emsp;The reproaches of those who reproach you have fallen on me.  
<sup>10</sup> When I wept and I fasted,  
&emsp;that was to my reproach.  
<sup>11</sup> When I made sackcloth my clothing,  
&emsp;I became a byword to them.  
<sup>12</sup> Those who sit in the gate talk about me.  
&emsp;I am the song of the drunkards.  
<sup>13</sup> But as for me, my prayer is to you, LORD, in an acceptable time.  
&emsp;God, in the abundance of your loving kindness, answer me in the truth of your salvation.  
<sup>14</sup> Deliver me out of the mire, and don’t let me sink.  
&emsp;Let me be delivered from those who hate me, and out of the deep waters.  
<sup>15</sup> Don’t let the flood waters overwhelm me,  
&emsp;neither let the deep swallow me up.  
&emsp;Don’t let the pit shut its mouth on me.  
<sup>16</sup> Answer me, LORD, for your loving kindness is good.  
&emsp;According to the multitude of your tender mercies, ~~turn to~~ <ins>look upon</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c251"></a>[c251](#c251)</sup> me.  
<sup>17</sup> Don’t hide your face from your servant,  
&emsp;for I am in distress.  
&emsp;Answer me speedily!  
<sup>18</sup> Draw near to my soul and redeem it.  
&emsp;Ransom me because of my enemies.  
<sup>19</sup> You know my reproach, my shame, and my dishonor.  
&emsp;My adversaries are all before you.  
<sup>20</sup> Reproach has broken my heart, and I am ~~full of heaviness~~ <ins>weak</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c253"></a>[c253](#c253)</sup>.  
&emsp;I looked for some to ~~take pity~~ <ins>grieve with me</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c252"></a>[c252](#c252)</sup>, but there was none;  
&emsp;for comforters, but I found none.  
<sup>21</sup> They also gave me ~~poison~~ <ins>gall</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c254"></a>[c254](#c254)</sup> for my food.  
&emsp;In my thirst, they gave me vinegar to drink.  
<sup>22</sup> Let their table before them become a snare.  
&emsp;May it become a retribution and a trap.  
<sup>23</sup> Let their eyes be darkened, so that they can’t see.  
&emsp;Let their backs be continually bent.  
<sup>24</sup> Pour out your indignation on them.  
&emsp;Let the fierceness of your anger overtake them.  
<sup>25</sup> Let their habitation be desolate.  
&emsp;Let no one dwell in their tents.  
<sup>26</sup> For they persecute him whom you have wounded.  
&emsp;They ~~tell of~~ <ins>add to</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c255"></a>[c255](#c255)</sup> the sorrow of those whom you have hurt.  
<sup>27</sup> Charge them with crime upon crime.  
&emsp;Don’t let them come into your righteousness.  
<sup>28</sup> Let them be blotted out of the book of life,  
&emsp;and not be written with the righteous.  
<sup>29</sup> But I am in pain and distress.  
&emsp;Let your salvation, God, protect me.  
<sup>30</sup> I will praise the name of God with a song,  
&emsp;and will magnify him with thanksgiving.  
<sup>31</sup> It will please the LORD better than an ox,  
&emsp;or a bull that has horns and hoofs.  
<sup>32</sup> The humble have seen it, and are glad.  
&emsp;You who seek after God, let your heart live.  
<sup>33</sup> For the LORD hears the needy,  
&emsp;and doesn’t despise his captive people.  
<sup>34</sup> Let heaven and earth praise him;  
&emsp;the seas, and everything that moves therein!  
<sup>35</sup> For God will save Zion, and build the cities of Judah.  
&emsp;They shall settle there, and own it.  
<sup>36</sup> The children also of his servants shall inherit it.  
&emsp;Those who love his name shall dwell therein.

## Psalm 70

*For the Chief Musician. By David. A reminder.*

<sup>1</sup> Hurry, God, to deliver me.  
&emsp;Come quickly to help me, LORD.  
<sup>2</sup> Let them be disappointed and confounded who seek my soul.  
&emsp;Let those who desire my ruin be turned back in disgrace.  
<sup>3</sup> Let them be turned because of their shame  
&emsp;who say, “Aha! Aha!”  
<sup>4</sup> Let all those who seek you rejoice and be glad in you.  
&emsp;Let those who love your salvation continually say,  
&emsp;“Let God be exalted!”  
<sup>5</sup> But I am poor and needy.  
&emsp;Come to me quickly, God.  
You are my help and my deliverer.  
&emsp;LORD, don’t delay.

## Psalm 71

<sup>1</sup> In you, LORD, I take refuge.  
&emsp;Never let me be disappointed.  
<sup>2</sup> Deliver me in your righteousness, and rescue me.  
&emsp;Turn your ear to me, and save me.  
<sup>3</sup> Be to me a rock of refuge to which I may always go.  
&emsp;Give the command to save me,  
&emsp;for you are my rock and my fortress.  
<sup>4</sup> Rescue me, my God, from the hand of the wicked,  
&emsp;from the hand of the unrighteous and ~~cruel~~ <ins>lawless</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c256"></a>[c256](#c256)</sup> man.  
<sup>5</sup> For you are my hope, Lord GOD,  
&emsp;my confidence from my youth.  
<sup>6</sup> I have relied on you from the womb.  
&emsp;You are he ~~who took me out of~~ <ins>who has supported me from</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c257"></a>[c257](#c257)</sup> my mother’s womb.  
&emsp;I will always praise you.  
<sup>7</sup> I am a marvel to many,  
&emsp;but you are my strong ~~refuge~~ <ins>helper</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c258"></a>[c258](#c258)</sup>.  
<sup>8</sup> My mouth shall be filled with your praise,  
&emsp;with your honor all day long.  
<sup>9</sup> Don’t reject me in my old age.  
&emsp;Don’t forsake me when my strength fails.  
<sup>10</sup> For my enemies talk about me.  
&emsp;Those who watch for my soul conspire together,  
&emsp;<sup>11</sup> saying, “God has forsaken him.  
&emsp;Pursue and take him, for no one will rescue him.”  
<sup>12</sup> God, don’t be far from me.  
&emsp;My God, hurry to help me.  
<sup>13</sup> Let my accusers be disappointed and consumed.  
&emsp;Let them be covered with disgrace and scorn who want to harm me.  
<sup>14</sup> But I will always hope,  
&emsp;and will add to all of your praise.  
<sup>15</sup> My mouth will tell about your righteousness,  
&emsp;and of your salvation all day,  
&emsp;though I don’t know its full measure.  
<sup>16</sup> I will come with the mighty acts of the Lord GOD.  
&emsp;I will make mention of your righteousness, even of yours alone.  
<sup>17</sup> God, you have taught me from my youth.  
&emsp;Until now, I have declared your wondrous works.  
<sup>18</sup> Yes, even when I am old and gray-haired, God, don’t forsake me,  
&emsp;until I have declared your strength to the next generation,  
&emsp;your might to everyone who is to come.  
<sup>19</sup> God, your righteousness also reaches to the heavens.  
&emsp;You have done great things.  
&emsp;God, who is like you?  
<sup>20</sup> You, who have shown us many and bitter troubles,  
&emsp;you will let me live.  
&emsp;You will bring us up again from the depths of the earth.  
<sup>21</sup> Increase my honor  
&emsp;and comfort me again.  
<sup>22</sup> I will also praise you with the harp for your faithfulness, my God.  
&emsp;I sing praises to you with the lyre, Holy One of Israel.  
<sup>23</sup> My lips shall shout for joy!  
&emsp;My soul, which you have redeemed, sings praises to you!  
<sup>24</sup> My tongue will also talk about your righteousness all day long,  
&emsp;for they are disappointed, and they are confounded,  
&emsp;who want to harm me.

## Psalm 72

*By Solomon.*

<sup>1</sup> God, give the king your justice;  
&emsp;your righteousness to the royal son.  
<sup>2</sup> He will judge your people with righteousness,  
&emsp;and your poor with justice.  
<sup>3</sup> The mountains shall bring ~~prosperity~~ <ins>peace</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c259"></a>[c259](#c259)</sup> to the people.  
&emsp;The hills bring the fruit of righteousness.  
<sup>4</sup> He will judge the poor of the people.  
&emsp;He will save the children of the needy,  
&emsp;and will break the oppressor in pieces.  
<sup>5</sup> They shall fear you while the sun endures;  
&emsp;and as long as the moon, throughout all generations.  
<sup>6</sup> He will come down like rain on the mown grass,  
&emsp;as showers that water the earth.  
<sup>7</sup> In his days, the righteous shall flourish,  
&emsp;and abundance of peace, until the moon is no more.  
<sup>8</sup> He shall have dominion also from sea to sea,  
&emsp;from the River to the ends of the earth.  
<sup>9</sup> Those who dwell in the wilderness shall bow before him.  
&emsp;His enemies shall lick the dust.  
<sup>10</sup> The kings of Tarshish and of the islands will bring tribute.  
&emsp;The kings of Sheba and Seba shall offer gifts.  
<sup>11</sup> Yes, all kings shall fall down before him.  
&emsp;All nations shall serve him.  
<sup>12</sup> For he will deliver the needy when he cries;  
&emsp;the poor, who has no helper.  
<sup>13</sup> He will have pity on the poor and needy.  
&emsp;He will save the souls of the ~~needy~~ <ins>poor</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c260"></a>[c260](#c260)</sup>.  
<sup>14</sup> He will redeem their soul from oppression and violence.  
&emsp;Their blood will be precious in his sight.  
<sup>15</sup> He will live; and Sheba’s gold will be given to him.  
&emsp;Men will pray for him continually.  
&emsp;They will bless him all day long.  
<sup>16</sup> Abundance of grain shall be throughout the land.  
&emsp;Its fruit sways like Lebanon.  
&emsp;Let it flourish, thriving like the grass of the field.  
<sup>17</sup> ~~His name endures forever.~~ <ins>May his name be blessed forever.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c261"></a>[c261](#c261)</sup>  
&emsp;His name continues as long as the sun.  
Men shall be blessed by him.  
&emsp;All nations will call him blessed.

<sup>18</sup> Praise be to the LORD God, the God of Israel,  
&emsp;who alone does marvelous deeds.  
<sup>19</sup> Blessed be his glorious name forever!  
&emsp;Let the whole earth be filled with his glory!  
Amen and amen.

<sup>20</sup> This ends the prayers by David, the son of Jesse.

## Psalm 73

**BOOK 3**

*A Psalm by Asaph.*

<sup>1</sup> Surely God[^29] is good to ~~Israel~~ <ins>the upright</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c262"></a>[c262](#c262)</sup>,  
&emsp;to those who are pure in heart.  
<sup>2</sup> But as for me, my feet were almost gone.  
&emsp;My steps had nearly slipped.  
<sup>3</sup> For I was envious of the arrogant,  
&emsp;when I saw the ~~prosperity~~ <ins>peace</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c263"></a>[c263](#c263)</sup> of the wicked.  
<sup>4</sup> For there are no struggles in their death,  
&emsp;but their strength is firm.  
<sup>5</sup> They are free from burdens of men,  
&emsp;neither are they plagued like other men.  
<sup>6</sup> Therefore pride is like a chain around their neck.  
&emsp;Violence covers them like a garment.  
<sup>7</sup> ~~Their eyes bulge with fat.~~ <ins>Their iniquity comes forth as from fatness.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c264"></a>[c264](#c264)</sup>  
&emsp;~~Their minds pass the limits of conceit.~~ <ins>The thoughts of their heart burst forth.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c265"></a>[c265](#c265)</sup>  
<sup>8</sup> They scoff and speak with malice.  
&emsp;~~In arrogance, they threaten oppression.~~ <ins>They speak iniquity from on high.</ins><sup>NV</sup><sup><a name="ref-c266"></a>[c266](#c266)</sup>  
<sup>9</sup> They have set their mouth in the heavens.  
&emsp;Their tongue walks through the earth.  
<sup>10</sup> Therefore their people return to them,  
&emsp;and they drink up waters of abundance.
